````math
If $`\lambda_{n}^{*}`$ is a global maximiser, a local maximiser, or a
stationary point of $`\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$, then
$`[\lambda_{n}^{*},\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{*})]`$ is, respectively, a
global maximiser, a local maximiser, or a stationary point of
$`\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})`$.

We now present the proof for these five properties. It is sufficient to
prove property
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty1 "Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
(i.e., the
([84](#chapterx1.A3.E84 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([85](#chapterx1.A3.E85 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))),
as properties
[0.C.2](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty2 "Property 0.C.2. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")-[0.C.5](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty5 "Property 0.C.5. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
can all be derived from property
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty1 "Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
by following the same steps outlined in Section
[IV-B](#S4.SS2 "IV-B Properties of LM-ELBO ‣ IV Locally Maximised ELBO for General Variational Inference Tasks ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
for the corresponding properties. Therefore, we refer to property
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty1 "Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
as the fundamental property.

We now prove the fundamental property
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty1 "Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"),
starting with
([84](#chapterx1.A3.E84 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
By comparing the definitions of
$`\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})`$ in
([35](#S5.E35 "In V-B Justification for use of LM-ELBO over Original ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and $`\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$ in
([39](#S5.E39 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
we observe that if
$`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})=q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s})`$, then
$`\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})=\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$.
Moreover, by the definition of $`\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n})`$ in Section
[V-C1](#S5.SS3.SSS1 "V-C1 Properties of local LM-ELBO ℒ_𝑠⁢(𝜆_𝑛) ‣ V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"),
we have
$`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})=q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))`$.
Therefore, setting $`\rho_{n}^{s}=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n})`$ gives
$`\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})=\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$,
i.e.,
$`\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})=\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))`$,
which proves
([84](#chapterx1.A3.E84 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

Next, we prove
([85](#chapterx1.A3.E85 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
Since
([84](#chapterx1.A3.E84 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
is already established, proving
([85](#chapterx1.A3.E85 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
is equivalent to proving:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))=\max_{\rho_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}).`$ |  | (89) |

To prove
([89](#chapterx1.A3.E89 "In Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
we introduce the following Lemma:

###### Lemma 0.C.1.

Recall the assumption from Section
[V-B](#S5.SS2 "V-B Justification for use of LM-ELBO over Original ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"),
where
$`q_{n}(\theta_{n};\rho_{n})=\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s})`$
and $`\rho_{n}=[\rho_{n}^{1},\rho_{n}^{2},\dots,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}}]`$. Let
$`\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n})`$ be the parameter value of
$`q_{n}(\theta_{n};\rho_{n})`$ that yields the optimal distribution
$`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})`$ in
([36](#S5.E36 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
with $`\lambda_{n}`$ held fixed, i.e.,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle q_{n}(\theta_{n};\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n}))=q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}),`$ |  | (90) |

then we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n})=[\rho_{n}^{1*}(\lambda_{n}),\rho_{n}^{2*}(\lambda_{n}),...,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}*}(\lambda_{n})],
``` |  |

where $`\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n})`$ $`(s=1,2,...,N_{s})`$ is defined in
Section
[V-C1](#S5.SS3.SSS1 "V-C1 Properties of local LM-ELBO ℒ_𝑠⁢(𝜆_𝑛) ‣ V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
as the parameter value that reproduces $`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})`$ in
([36](#S5.E36 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
with $`\lambda_{n}`$ held fixed, i.e.,
$`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})=q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))`$.

###### Proof.

Let $`\rho_{n}^{s,o}(\lambda_{n})`$ denote the value of $`\rho_{n}^{s}`$
for $`s=1,2,\dots,N_{s}`$ when
$`\rho_{n}=[\rho_{n}^{1},\rho_{n}^{2},\dots,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}}]`$ takes
the value $`\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n})`$. That is,
$`\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n})=[\rho_{n}^{1,o}(\lambda_{n}),\rho_{n}^{2,o}(\lambda_{n}),\dots,\rho_{n}^{N_{s},o}(\lambda_{n})]`$.
Then, we have
$`q_{n}(\theta_{n};\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n}))=\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s,o}(\lambda_{n}))`$.
Moreover, from
([90](#chapterx1.A3.E90 "In Lemma 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and the fact that
$`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})=\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})`$
as stated in
([36](#S5.E36 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
we know that
$`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n};\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n}))=\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})`$.
Therefore, we have
$`\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s,o}(\lambda_{n}))=\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})`$.
Subsequently, by marginalising $`\theta_{n}^{s-}`$ from both sides, it
follows that
$`q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s,o}(\lambda_{n}))=q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})`$
for each $`s=1,2,...,N_{s}`$. This implies that each
$`\rho_{n}^{s,o}(\lambda_{n})`$ is also the optimal parameter value that
reproduces $`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})`$ with $`\lambda_{n}`$ held
fixed. This matches the definition of $`\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n})`$ in
Section
[V-C1](#S5.SS3.SSS1 "V-C1 Properties of local LM-ELBO ℒ_𝑠⁢(𝜆_𝑛) ‣ V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"),
where
$`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})=q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))`$.
Therefore, we conclude that
$`\rho_{n}^{s,o}(\lambda_{n})=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n})`$ for
$`s=1,2,\dots,N_{s}`$, and thus
$`\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n})=[\rho_{n}^{1*}(\lambda_{n}),\rho_{n}^{2*}(\lambda_{n}),...,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}*}(\lambda_{n})]`$.

∎

We now prove
([89](#chapterx1.A3.E89 "In Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
As mentioned in Section
[V-C1](#S5.SS3.SSS1 "V-C1 Properties of local LM-ELBO ℒ_𝑠⁢(𝜆_𝑛) ‣ V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"),
the LM-ELBO $`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})`$ naturally possesses the
properties described in Section
[IV-B](#S4.SS2 "IV-B Properties of LM-ELBO ‣ IV Locally Maximised ELBO for General Variational Inference Tasks ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
owing to its derivation, where Property 1 states that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}=\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n}))=\max_{\rho_{n}}\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}),`$ |  | (91) |

where $`\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n})`$ denotes the parameter value that
yields the optimal distribution $`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})`$, as defined in
Lemma
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").
Using Lemma
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"),
we have
$`\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}=\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n}))=\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}=[\rho_{n}^{1*}(\lambda_{n}),\rho_{n}^{2*}(\lambda_{n}),...,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}*}(\lambda_{n})])`$
where $`\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n})`$ $`(s=1,2,\dots,N_{s})`$ is the
optimal parameter value of $`q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s})`$ such
that
$`q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})=q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))`$,
as defined in Section
[V-C1](#S5.SS3.SSS1 "V-C1 Properties of local LM-ELBO ℒ_𝑠⁢(𝜆_𝑛) ‣ V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").
Furthermore, using the relation
$`\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n})=\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})`$
($`\rho_{n}=[\rho_{n}^{1},\rho_{n}^{2},\dots,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}}]`$) from
the expression above
([35](#S5.E35 "In V-B Justification for use of LM-ELBO over Original ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
we obtain:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}=`$ | $`\displaystyle\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n}))=\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}=[\rho_{n}^{1*}(\lambda_{n}),\rho_{n}^{2*}(\lambda_{n}),...,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}*}(\lambda_{n})])`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n})).`$ |  | (92) |

Additionally, using
$`\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n})=\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})`$
with $`\rho_{n}=[\rho_{n}^{1},\rho_{n}^{2},...,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}}]`$
again, we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\max_{\rho_{n}}\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\max_{\rho_{n}^{1},\rho_{n}^{2},...,\rho_{n}^{N_{s}}}\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\max_{\rho_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}).`$ |  | (93) |

Next, by combining
([91](#chapterx1.A3.E91 "In Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([92](#chapterx1.A3.E92 "In Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\max_{\rho_{n}}\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}=\rho_{n}^{*}(\lambda_{n}))`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\max_{\rho_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}).`$ |  | (94) |

The equality in
([94](#chapterx1.A3.E94 "In Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
holds if and only if
$`\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))=\max_{\rho_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})`$
for all $`s=1,2,\dots,N_{s}`$. Since
([93](#chapterx1.A3.E93 "In Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
implies that this equality holds, we conclude that
$`\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s}=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}))=\max_{\rho_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})`$
for all $`s=1,2,...,N_{s}`$, thus proving
([89](#chapterx1.A3.E89 "In Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

Since we have proven both
([84](#chapterx1.A3.E84 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([89](#chapterx1.A3.E89 "In Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
it follows that
([85](#chapterx1.A3.E85 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
is also true, thus completing the proof of the fundamental property
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty1 "Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
(i.e.,
([84](#chapterx1.A3.E84 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([85](#chapterx1.A3.E85 "In Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")))
for $`\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$ and
$`\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n},\rho_{n}^{s})`$. Recall that properties
[0.C.2](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty2 "Property 0.C.2. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")–[0.C.5](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty5 "Property 0.C.5. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
can all be derived from the fundamental property
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty1 "Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
by following the same steps as in Section
[IV-B](#S4.SS2 "IV-B Properties of LM-ELBO ‣ IV Locally Maximised ELBO for General Variational Inference Tasks ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
for their corresponding properties. Therefore, we conclude that all
properties
[0.C.1](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty1 "Property 0.C.1. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")–[0.C.5](#chapterx1.A3.Thmproperty5 "Property 0.C.5. ‣ Appendix 0.C Properties of local LM-ELBO and proofs ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
are valid.

## Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient

In this appendix, we derive the gradient
$`{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ as
presented in Section
[VI-A2](#S6.SS1.SSS2 "VI-A2 Derivation of 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").
Using the property in
([40](#S5.E40 "In V-C1 Properties of local LM-ELBO ℒ_𝑠⁢(𝜆_𝑛) ‣ V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
we have
$`\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})\!=\!\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s})|_{\rho_{n}^{s}=\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s})}`$.
To compute this, we first take the partial derivative of
$`\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s})`$ with respect to
$`\lambda_{n}^{s}`$, and then substitute $`\rho_{n}^{s}`$ with
$`\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$. More compactly, we can express this
as
$`\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})\!=\!\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))`$,
where $`\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ is treated as independent of
$`\lambda_{n}^{s}`$ during the gradient evaluation. We now evaluate this
gradient
$`\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))`$.
Using
([35](#S5.E35 "In V-B Justification for use of LM-ELBO over Original ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and the fact that
$`q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})\!=\!q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))`$
(as stated immediately prior to Section
[VI-A](#S6.SS1 "VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})+\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))}\log\frac{p(\theta_{n}^{s}|M_{n}^{s})}{q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))}`$ |  | (95) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})}\log\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})-\frac{1}{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})}\log q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})+\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log\frac{p(\theta_{n}^{s}|M_{n}^{s})}{q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}-\frac{1}{N_{s}}\text{KL}(q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})||\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})),`$ |  | (96) |

where the KL divergence is defined as
$`\text{KL}(q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})||\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n}))=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})}\log\frac{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})}{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})}`$.
To compute
$`{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))`$,
where $`\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ (and thus
$`q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})=q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s};\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))`$)
are treated as independent of $`\lambda_{n}^{s}`$ during the gradient
evaluation, we have

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))=`$ | $`\displaystyle-{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\text{KL}(q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})||\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n}))+{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})`$ |  | (97) |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})+{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s}),`$ |  | (98) |

where
$`\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s}),\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$
are defined as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=`$ | $`\displaystyle-\frac{1}{N_{s}}\text{KL}(q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})||\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n}))=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})},`$ |  | (99) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n}),`$ |  | (100) |

and we note that $`q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})`$ is treated as
independent of $`\lambda_{n}^{s}`$ during the gradient evaluation.

Recall from Section
[VI-A1](#S6.SS1.SSS1 "VI-A1 Prediction and update steps ‣ VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
and the opening paragraph of Section
[VI-A](#S6.SS1 "VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
that at sensor node $`s`$, the predictive prior is
$`\hat{p}(X_{n})=\prod_{k=1}^{K}\hat{p}(X_{n,k})`$ with
$`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n,k})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})`$,
and
$`q_{n}(X_{n};\!\lambda_{n}^{s})\!=\!\prod_{k=1}^{K}\!q_{n}(X_{n,k};\!\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$
with
$`q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})\!=\!\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n})`$.
Then, using the multivariate Gaussian KL divergence formula, the
$`\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ in
([99](#chapterx1.A4.E99 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
is

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=-\frac{1}{N_{s}}\text{KL}(q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})||\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n}))=-\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\text{KL}(q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})||\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n,k}))`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{-1}{2N_{s}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[\mathrm{Tr}\left((\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}\right)+(\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{\top}(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}(\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})-\log|\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}|+\log|\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}|-d\right]`$ |  | (101) |

To compute the $`\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ in
([100](#chapterx1.A4.E100 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
first we derive the inner expectation
$`\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})`$.
The detailed derivation of follows the same steps as in Equations
(67)-(70) of Appendix E in \[[1](#biba.bib1)\], so we will not repeat it
here and instead provide the final form:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})=\sum_{k=1}^{K}\log\mathcal{N}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s};HX_{n,k},\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})+C_{x}^{s}`$ |  | (102) |

where $`C_{x}^{s}`$ is a constant that does not depend on $`X_{n}`$, and
pseudo-measurement
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
and covariance
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
at each sensor $`s`$ are

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{R_{k}^{s}}{\sum_{j=1}^{M_{n}^{s}}q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n,j}^{s}=k)},`$ |  | (103) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{M_{n}^{s}}Y_{n,j}^{s}q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n,j}^{s}=k)}{\sum_{j=1}^{M_{n}^{s}}q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n,j}^{s}=k)}.`$ |  | (104) |

Subsequently, the $`\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ in
([100](#chapterx1.A4.E100 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
is given by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})q_{n}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{K}\log\mathcal{N}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s};HX_{n,k},\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})+C_{x}^{s}\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=C_{x}^{s}+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[-\frac{1}{2}\log|\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}|-\frac{d}{2}\log 2\pi-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}-HX_{n,k})^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}-HX_{n,k})\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(H\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}(H\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{Tr}\left(H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}H\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}\right)+C_{xy}^{s}`$ |  | (105) |

where
$`C_{xy}^{s}=C_{x}^{s}-\frac{1}{2}\log\prod_{k=1}^{K}|\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}|-\frac{dK}{2}\log 2\pi`$
is a constant term that does not depend on
$`\lambda_{n}^{s}=[\lambda_{n,1}^{s},\lambda_{n,2}^{s},...,\lambda_{n,K}^{s}]`$,
with $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}=[\mu^{k,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}]`$
($`k=1,2,...,K`$) as defined in
([45](#S6.E45 "In VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

Finally, the gradient of the local LM-ELBO
$`{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))`$
can be written as follows using
([98](#chapterx1.A4.E98 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([101](#chapterx1.A4.E101 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([105](#chapterx1.A4.E105 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{F}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s},\rho_{n}^{s*}(\lambda_{n}^{s}))={\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})+{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{2N_{s}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\left[\log|\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}|-\mathrm{Tr}\left((\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}\right)-(\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{\top}(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}(\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\nabla_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\left[(H\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}(H\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})+\mathrm{Tr}\left(H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}H\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}\right)\right],`$ |  |

where
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
and
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
are given in
([48](#S6.E48 "In VI-A2 Derivation of 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
and are treated as independent of $`\lambda_{n}^{s}`$ during gradient
evaluation. The gradients
$`{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ can then
be computed with respect to local estimates of each variational
parameter $`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}`$ and $`\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}`$, $`k=1,...,K`$,
using the matrix derivative formulas in \[[4](#biba.bib4)\], i.e.,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{N_{s}}(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}(\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}-\mu^{k,s}_{n|n})+H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}-H\mu^{k,s}_{n|n})`$ |  | (106) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2N_{s}}\left((\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\right)-\frac{1}{2}H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}H`$ |  | (107) |

## Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient

#### 0.E.1  The exponential family and some properties

The general form of canonical exponential family distributions can be
expressed as follows,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
q(x;\lambda)=h(x)\exp\left(\lambda^{\top}T(x)-A(\lambda)\right)
``` |  | (108) |

where $`x`$ is the random variable, $`h(x)`$ is the base function,
$`\lambda`$ is the natural parameter of the distribution. $`T(x)`$ is
the sufficient statistic, and $`A(\lambda)`$ is the log partition
function that ensures $`q(x;\lambda)`$ integrating to $`1`$. This
general form covers a wide range of probability distributions, including
the Gaussian, Poisson, and Binomial distributions. Taking the
multivariate Gaussian distribution $`\mathcal{N}(x;\mu,\Sigma)`$ as an
example, the exponential family components defined in
([108](#chapterx1.A5.E108 "In 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
are

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle h(x)=(2\pi)^{-\frac{d}{2}},\quad T(x)=\begin{bmatrix}x\\
xx^{\top}\end{bmatrix}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lambda=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{1}\\
\lambda_{2}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\Sigma^{-1}\mu\\
-\frac{1}{2}\Sigma^{-1}\end{bmatrix}`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle A(\lambda)=-\frac{1}{4}(\lambda_{1})^{\top}(\lambda_{2})^{-1}\lambda_{1}-\frac{1}{2}\log|\!-\!2\lambda_{2}|`$ |  |

A useful property is that the expectation of the natural sufficient
statistics is the gradient the log-partition function $`A(\lambda)`$
with respect to the natural parameter $`\lambda`$:

|     |                                                            |     |       |
|-----|------------------------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       \mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}[T(x)]=\nabla_{\lambda}A(\lambda)
       ```                                                         |     | (109) |

Another useful property is that the covariance matrix of the sufficient
statistics $`T(x)`$ is the Hessian of the log-partition function
$`A(\lambda)`$ with respect to the natural parameter $`\lambda`$.

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}\left[\left(T(x)-\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}[T(x)]\right)\left(T(x)-\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}[T(x)]\right)^{\top}\right]=\nabla_{\lambda}^{2}A(\lambda).
``` |  | (110) |

Subsequently, the Fisher information matrix $`G(\lambda)`$ is also the
Hessian of the log-partition function $`A(\lambda)`$, i.e.,

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle G(\lambda)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}\left[\left(\nabla_{\lambda}\log{p(X;\lambda)}\right)\left(\nabla_{\lambda}\log{p(X;\lambda)}\right)^{\top}\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}\left[\left(T(x)-\nabla_{\lambda}A(\lambda)\right)\left(T(x)-\nabla_{\lambda}A(\lambda)\right)^{\top}\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}\left[\left(T(x)-\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}[T(x)]\right)\left(T(x)-\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}[T(x)]\right)^{\top}\right]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\nabla^{2}_{\lambda}A(\lambda),`$ |  | (111) |

where the second last line is obtained by using
([109](#chapterx1.A5.E109 "In 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
and the last line is obtained by using
([110](#chapterx1.A5.E110 "In 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

##### 0.E.11 Natural gradient and the expectation parameter

A useful strategy used in this paper to avoid the computation of the
inversion of Fisher information matrix is the variable transformation
\[[5](#biba.bib5)\]. This allows the natural gradient with respect to
the natural parameters to be computed via the gradient with respect to
the expectation of the sufficient statistics.

Specifically, let the parameter $`m`$ denote the expectation of the
sufficient statistics. Then,
([109](#chapterx1.A5.E109 "In 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
defines a mapping between $`\lambda`$ and $`m`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle m=\mathrm{E}_{q(x;\lambda)}[T(x)]=\nabla_{\lambda}A(\lambda).`$ |  | (112) |

For an exponential family in a minimal representation (commonly used and
applicable in this paper), there exists a one-to-one mapping between the
natural parameter $`\lambda`$ and the expectation parameter $`m`$ (see
\[[5](#biba.bib5)\] for details). Thus one can derive a unique reverse
mapping from
([112](#chapterx1.A5.E112 "In 0.E.11 Natural gradient and the expectation parameter ‣ 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and express $`f(\lambda)`$ in terms of $`m`$. Subsequently, for a
function $`f(\lambda)`$ of the natural parameter $`\lambda`$, its
gradient $`\nabla_{\lambda}f(\lambda)`$ can be related to its gradient
with respect to the sufficient statistics expectation parameter $`m`$ as
follows:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\lambda}f(\lambda)=(\mathbf{J}_{\lambda}m)\nabla_{m}f(m)=\nabla^{2}_{\lambda}A(\lambda)\nabla_{m}f(m)=G(\lambda)\nabla_{m}f(m)`$ |  | (113) |

where $`f(m)`$ is the is the reparameterised form of $`f(\lambda)`$
using the reverse relationship in
([112](#chapterx1.A5.E112 "In 0.E.11 Natural gradient and the expectation parameter ‣ 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
$`\mathbf{J}_{\lambda}m`$ is the Jacobian matrix of $`m`$ with respect
to $`\lambda`$, arising from the application of the chain rule. The last
two equalities follow from
([112](#chapterx1.A5.E112 "In 0.E.11 Natural gradient and the expectation parameter ‣ 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([111](#chapterx1.A5.E111 "In 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

Finally, using the definition of the natural gradient
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda}f(\lambda)=G(\lambda)^{-1}\nabla_{\lambda}f(\lambda)`$
, we observe an important property: the natural gradient with respect to
natural parameter equals to the gradient with respect to the sufficient
statistics expectation parameter:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda}f(\lambda)=\nabla_{m}f(m),`$ |  | (114) |

Thus, the Fisher information matrix is no longer required in the natural
gradient computation. This variable transformation will be applied in
the next section to simplify the natural gradient calculation.

#### 0.E.2 Calculate the natural gradients

In the following, we will compute the natural gradient
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$ as
presented in Section
[VI-B2](#S6.SS2.SSS2 "VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").

Recall from Section
[VI-B2](#S6.SS2.SSS2 "VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
that both the predictive prior and variational distribution at sensor
$`s`$ are independent Gaussian distributions:
$`\hat{p}(X_{n})=\prod_{k=1}^{K}\hat{p}(X_{n,k};\eta_{n,k}^{s})`$ and
$`q_{n}(X_{n};\!\lambda_{n}^{s})\!=\!\prod_{k=1}^{K}\!q_{n}(X_{n,k};\!\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$,
expressed in the exponential family form:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n,k};\eta_{n,k}^{s})=h(X_{n,k})\exp\left({\eta_{n,k}^{s}}^{\top}T(X_{n,k})-A(\eta_{n,k}^{s})\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=h(X_{n,k})\exp\left({\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}^{\top}T(X_{n,k})-A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})\right)`$ |  |

where $`\eta_{n,k}^{s}`$ and $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}`$ are the natural
parameters of $`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n,k};\eta_{n,k}^{s})`$ and
$`q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$, respectively. Since both are
Gaussian distributions, they share the same base function
$`h(X_{n,k})`$, sufficient statistics $`T(X_{n,k})`$, and log partition
function $`A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$. Additionally, the sufficient
statistics expectation parameter
$`m_{n,k}^{s}=\mathrm{E}_{q(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})}T(X_{n,k})`$ is
defined in Section
[VI-B2](#S6.SS2.SSS2 "VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").
The relationship between the expectation parameter $`m_{n,k}^{s}`$, the
natural parameter $`\eta_{n,k}^{s},\lambda_{n,k}^{s}`$, and the Gaussian
mean and covariance are given in
([53](#S6.E53 "In VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([58](#S6.E58 "In VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
summarised below:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\eta_{n,k}^{s}=\begin{bmatrix}\eta_{n,k}^{s,1}\\
\eta_{n,k}^{s,2}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}\\
-\frac{1}{2}(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\end{bmatrix},\quad\lambda_{n,k}^{s}=\begin{bmatrix}\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}\\
\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}(\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n})^{-1}\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}\\
-\frac{1}{2}(\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n})^{-1}\end{bmatrix},\quad m_{n,k}^{s}=\begin{bmatrix}m_{n,k}^{s,1}\\
m_{n,k}^{s,2}\end{bmatrix}=\begin{bmatrix}\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}\\
\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}[\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}]^{\top}+\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}\end{bmatrix}`$ |  | (115) |

We now begin the computation. Using
([98](#chapterx1.A4.E98 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and the natural gradient definition from
([51](#S6.E51 "In VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
we have

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})+\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s}),`$ |  | (116) |

where
$`\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s}),\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$
are given in
([99](#chapterx1.A4.E99 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([100](#chapterx1.A4.E100 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
respectively:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})},`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n}),`$ |  |

with $`q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n}^{s})`$ treated treated as independent of
$`\lambda_{n}^{s}`$ during the (natural) gradient evaluation.

We will now first compute
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$.
Note that

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n}^{s})=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})}\log p_{n}(X_{n,k};\eta_{n,k}^{s}))-\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})}\log q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s}))\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})}\left({\eta_{n,k}^{s}}^{\top}T(X_{n,k})-A(\eta_{n,k}^{s})\right)-\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})}\left({\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}^{\top}T(X_{n,k})-A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})\right)\right]`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\left[({\eta_{n,k}^{s}}^{\top}-{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}^{\top})\nabla_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})+A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})-A(\eta_{n,k}^{s})\right]`$ |  |

where the last line uses the property in
([109](#chapterx1.A5.E109 "In 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
For each component $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}`$, the gradient can then be
calculated as

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\left(\nabla^{2}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}){\eta_{n,k}^{s}}-\nabla^{2}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}){\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}-\nabla_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})+\nabla_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})\right)`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\nabla^{2}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}A(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})({\eta_{n,k}^{s}}-{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}})`$ |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}G(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})({\eta_{n,k}^{s}}-{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}),`$ |  |

where the property in
([111](#chapterx1.A5.E111 "In 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
is used to obtain the last line. Consequently, the natural gradient is
given by:
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=G(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})^{-1}\nabla_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=\frac{1}{N_{s}}(\eta_{n,k}^{s}-\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$.
Then, according to
([115](#chapterx1.A5.E115 "In 0.E.2 Calculate the natural gradients ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
each component of the natural gradient has the following form:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=\frac{1}{N_{s}}(\eta_{n,k}^{s,1}-\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1})=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\left[(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}-(\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n})^{-1}\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}\right]\\
&\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=\frac{1}{N_{s}}(\eta_{n,k}^{s,2}-\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2})=\frac{1}{2N_{s}}\left[(\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n})^{-1}-(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\right]\end{aligned}`$ |  | (117) |

Next, to compute
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n}^{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n}^{s})`$,
we rewrite the expression of $`\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$
given in
([105](#chapterx1.A4.E105 "In Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
in terms of $`m_{n,k}^{s}`$, using the relationship in
([115](#chapterx1.A5.E115 "In 0.E.2 Calculate the natural gradients ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")):

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(m_{n,k}^{s})`$ | $`\displaystyle=C_{xy}^{s}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{Tr}\left(H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}H\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}\right)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(H\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}(H\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}-\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})`$ |  | (118) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=C_{xy}^{s}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}\mathrm{Tr}\left(H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}H(m_{n,k}^{s,2}-m_{n,k}^{s,1}(m_{n,k}^{s,1})^{\top})\right)-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(Hm_{n,k}^{s,1}-\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}(Hm_{n,k}^{s,1}-\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}),`$ |  |  |

where
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
and
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
are given in
([48](#S6.E48 "In VI-A2 Derivation of 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
and are treated as independent of $`\lambda_{n}^{s}`$ during gradient
evaluation. Subsequently, applying the matrix derivative formulas in
\[[4](#biba.bib4)\], the gradients with respect to the expectation
parameters $`m_{n,k}^{s,1}`$ and $`m_{n,k}^{s,2}`$ are

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle{\nabla}_{m_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(m_{n,k}^{s})`$ | $`\displaystyle=H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$ |  | (119) |

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle{\nabla}_{m_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(m_{n,k}^{s})`$ | $`\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}H`$ |  | (120) |

Using the property in
([114](#chapterx1.A5.E114 "In 0.E.11 Natural gradient and the expectation parameter ‣ 0.E.1 The exponential family and some properties ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
these correspond to the required natural gradients:
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})={\nabla}_{m_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(m_{n,k}^{s})`$,
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})={\nabla}_{m_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{2}(m_{n,k}^{s})`$.
Finally, as indicated in
([116](#chapterx1.A5.E116 "In 0.E.2 Calculate the natural gradients ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
combining these results with the derived
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$,
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}^{1}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$
from
([117](#chapterx1.A5.E117 "In 0.E.2 Calculate the natural gradients ‣ Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
yields the overall natural gradients
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$,
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$
as shown in
([62](#S6.E62 "In VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([63](#S6.E63 "In VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

## Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes

#### 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS)

Recall from
([45](#S6.E45 "In VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
that the local estimate of the optimised variational parameter is
defined as
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}=[\mu^{k,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}],k=1,...,K`$ for
both DeG-VT-DS and DeG-VT-GT algorithms. For DeG-VT-DS algorithm, the
update of $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}`$ for jointly optimising the LM-ELBO
$`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})`$ follows
([41](#S5.E41 "In V-D1 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with diminishing stepsize ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
Specifically, the update equation for each parameter estimate
$`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}`$ and $`\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}`$, $`k=1,...,K`$, at
iteration $`i`$ and each sensor $`s`$, is given by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\mu^{k,j}_{n|n}(i)+\alpha_{i}\nabla_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i))`$ |  | (121) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\Sigma^{k,j}_{n|n}(i)+\alpha_{i}\nabla_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i))`$ |  | (122) |

where each gradient component is derived in detail in Appendix
[0.D](#chapterx1.A4 "Appendix 0.D Derivation of the gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
(and briefly outlined in Section
[VI-A2](#S6.SS1.SSS2 "VI-A2 Derivation of 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i))`$ | $`\displaystyle={\nabla}_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})|_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}=\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i)}=\frac{1}{N_{s}}(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}(\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i)-\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})+H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s})^{-1}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}(i)-H\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i))`$ |  | (123) |

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i))`$ | $`\displaystyle={\nabla}_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s})|_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}=\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i)}=\frac{1}{2N_{s}}\left((\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i))^{-1}-(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\right)-\frac{1}{2}H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}(i))^{-1}H`$ |  | (124) |

The local pseudo-measurement
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
and covariance
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
in
([123](#chapterx1.A6.E123 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([124](#chapterx1.A6.E124 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
at each sensor $`s`$ are given by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}(i)=`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{R_{k}^{s}}{\sum_{j=1}^{M_{n}^{s}}q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n,j}^{s}=k)},\ \ \ \ \ \mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}(i)=\frac{\sum_{j=1}^{M_{n}^{s}}Y_{n,j}^{s}q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n,j}^{s}=k)}{\sum_{j=1}^{M_{n}^{s}}q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n,j}^{s}=k)},`$ |  | (125) |

where $`q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n,j}^{s})`$ is computed using the most
recent local estimate $`\lambda_{n}^{s}(i)`$ as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle q_{n}^{s,*}(\theta_{n,j}^{s})\propto\frac{\Lambda_{0}^{s}}{V^{s}}\delta[\theta_{n,j}^{s}=0]+\sum_{k=1}^{K}\Lambda_{k}^{s}l_{k}^{s}\delta[\theta_{n,j}^{s}=k],`$ |  | (126) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle l_{k}^{s}=\mathcal{N}(Y_{n,j}^{s};H\mu_{n|n}^{k,s}(i),R_{k}^{s})\text{exp}(-0.5\text{Tr}({(R_{k}^{s})}^{-1}H\Sigma_{n|n}^{k,s}(i)H^{\top})).`$ |  | (127) |

The full procedure of DeG-VT-DS, including prediction and update steps,
can be seen in Algorithm
[1](#alg1 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").

Input:
$`q^{*}_{n-1}(X_{n-1,k};\lambda_{n-1,k}^{s})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n-1,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n-1|n-1},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n-1|n-1})`$,
$`k=1,...,K`$, $`Y_{n}^{s}`$, maximum iteration $`I_{max}`$.

1 Output:
$`q^{*}_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})=\prod_{k=1}^{K}q^{*}_{n,k}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n})`$.

2 for *$`k=1,2,...,K`$* do

    3 Prediction step:
$`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n,k})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})`$
using
([10](#S2.E10 "In II-C1 Prediction step in the variational filtering ‣ II-C Variational Filtering for Multi-object Tracking ‣ II Problem Formulation and Modelling ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

4 Initialisation: For $`k=1,2,...,K`$, set
$`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(0)=\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}`$,
$`\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(0)=\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}`$.

5 for *$`i=0,1,...,I_{max}`$* do

    6 Exchange variables $`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i),\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i)`$
$`(k=1,2,...,K)`$ with the current neighbors of sensor $`s`$ in
$`\mathcal{N}_{s}(i)`$.

    7 For $`j=1,...,M_{n}`$, compute $`q^{s,*}_{n}(\theta_{n,j})`$ using
([126](#chapterx1.A6.E126 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

    8 Compute the gradients
$`\nabla_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}},\nabla_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}}`$ in
([123](#chapterx1.A6.E123 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([124](#chapterx1.A6.E124 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

    9 for *$`k=1,2,...,K`$* do

       10 Update $`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i+1)`$, $`\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i+1)`$
according to
([121](#chapterx1.A6.E121 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([122](#chapterx1.A6.E122 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([42](#S5.E42 "In V-D1 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with diminishing stepsize ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

11 After convergence,
$`q^{*}_{n,k}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n})`$,
where $`\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n}`$ are the final updates of
$`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i),\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i)`$.

Algorithm 1 DeG-VT-DS at time step $`n`$ for each sensor $`s`$

#### 0.F.2 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with gradient tracking (DeG-VT-GT)

Recall from
([45](#S6.E45 "In VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
that the local estimate of the optimised variational parameter is
defined as
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}=[\mu^{k,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}],k=1,...,K`$ for
both DeG-VT-DS and DeG-VT-GT algorithms. For DeG-VT-GT algorithm, the
update of $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}`$ and gradient estimates
$`\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s}=[\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,1},\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,2}]`$ for
jointly optimising the LM-ELBO $`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})`$ follows
([43](#S5.E43 "In V-D2 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with gradient tracking ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([44](#S5.E44 "In V-D2 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with gradient tracking ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
Specifically, the update equation for each parameter estimate
$`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}`$, $`\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}`$ and gradient estimates
$`\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,1},\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,2}`$ $`k=1,...,K`$, at
iteration $`i`$ and each sensor $`s`$, are given by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\mu^{k,j}_{n|n}(i)+\alpha\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,1}(i),`$ |  | (128) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\Sigma^{k,j}_{n|n}(i)+\alpha\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,2}(i).`$ |  | (129) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,1}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{j,1}(i)+\nabla_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i+1))-\nabla_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i)),`$ |  | (130) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,2}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{j,2}(i)+\nabla_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i+1))-\nabla_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i)).`$ |  | (131) |

The full procedure of DeG-VT-GT, including prediction and update steps,
can be seen in Algorithm
[2](#alg2 "In 0.F.2 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with gradient tracking (DeG-VT-GT) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").

Input:
$`q^{*}_{n-1}(X_{n-1,k};\lambda_{n-1,k}^{s})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n-1,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n-1|n-1},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n-1|n-1})`$,
$`k=1,...,K`$, $`Y_{n}^{s}`$, maximum iteration $`I_{max}`$.

1 Output:
$`q^{*}_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})=\prod_{k=1}^{K}q^{*}_{n,k}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=\prod_{k=1}^{K}\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n})`$.

2 for *$`k=1,2,...,K`$* do

    3 Prediction step:
$`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n,k})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})`$
using
([10](#S2.E10 "In II-C1 Prediction step in the variational filtering ‣ II-C Variational Filtering for Multi-object Tracking ‣ II Problem Formulation and Modelling ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

4 Initialisation: For $`k=1,2,...,K`$, set
$`\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n}(0)=\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}`$,
$`\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n}(0)=\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}`$,
$`\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,1}(0)=\nabla_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(0))`$,
$`\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,2}(0)=\nabla_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(0))`$.

5 for *$`i=0,1,...,I_{max}`$* do

    6 Exchange variables $`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i),\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i)`$,
$`\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s}(i)`$ $`(k=1,2,...,K)`$ with the current neighbors
of sensor $`s`$ in $`\mathcal{N}_{s}(i)`$.

    7 For $`j=1,...,M_{n}`$, compute $`q^{s,*}_{n}(\theta_{n,j})`$ using
([126](#chapterx1.A6.E126 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

    8 Compute the gradients
$`\nabla_{\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}},\nabla_{\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}}`$ in
([123](#chapterx1.A6.E123 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([124](#chapterx1.A6.E124 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

    9 for *$`k=1,2,...,K`$* do

       10 Update $`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i+1)`$, $`\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i+1)`$
according to
([128](#chapterx1.A6.E128 "In 0.F.2 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with gradient tracking (DeG-VT-GT) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([129](#chapterx1.A6.E129 "In 0.F.2 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with gradient tracking (DeG-VT-GT) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([42](#S5.E42 "In V-D1 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with diminishing stepsize ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

       11 Update $`\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,1}(i+1),\bm{\xi}_{n,k}^{s,2}(i+1)`$
according to
([130](#chapterx1.A6.E130 "In 0.F.2 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with gradient tracking (DeG-VT-GT) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([131](#chapterx1.A6.E131 "In 0.F.2 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with gradient tracking (DeG-VT-GT) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([42](#S5.E42 "In V-D1 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with diminishing stepsize ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

12 After convergence,
$`q^{*}_{n,k}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n})`$,
where $`\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n}`$ are the final updates of
$`\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}(i),\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i)`$.

Algorithm 2 DeG-VT-GT at time step $`n`$ for each sensor $`s`$

#### 0.F.3 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeNG-VT-DS)

Recall from
([45](#S6.E45 "In VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
that, for both DeNG-VT-DS and DeNG-VT-GT algorithms, the local estimate
of the optimised variational parameter is defined as
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}=[\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1},\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}]`$
($`k=1,...,K`$), with each $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1},\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}`$
defined in
([53](#S6.E53 "In VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
For DeG-VT-DS algorithm, the update of $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}`$ for jointly
optimising the LM-ELBO $`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})`$ follows
([41](#S5.E41 "In V-D1 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with diminishing stepsize ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
Specifically, the update equation for each parameter estimate
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1},\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}`$, $`k=1,...,K`$, at iteration
$`i`$ and each sensor $`s`$, is given by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\lambda_{n,k}^{j,1}(i)+\alpha_{i}\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i))`$ |  | (132) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\lambda_{n,k}^{j,2}(i)+\alpha_{i}\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n}^{s}(i))`$ |  | (133) |

where each natural gradient component is derived in detail in Appendix
[0.E](#chapterx1.A5 "Appendix 0.E Derivation of the natural gradient ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")
(and briefly outlined in Section
[VI-B2](#S6.SS2.SSS2 "VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(i))`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N_{s}}\left[(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}-(\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i))^{-1}\mu^{k,s}_{n|n}\right]+H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}(i))^{-1}\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}(i)`$ |  | (134) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(i))`$ | $`\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2N_{s}}[(\Sigma^{k,s}_{n|n}(i))^{-1}-(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}]-\frac{1}{2}H^{\top}(\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}(i))^{-1}H`$ |  | (135) |

where local pseudo-measurement
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muY\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
and covariance
$`\mkern 3.5mu\overline{\mkern-3.5muR\mkern-3.5mu}\mkern 3.5mu_{n}^{k,s}`$
at each sensor $`s`$ have the same form as in
([125](#chapterx1.A6.E125 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

The full procedure of DeNG-VT-DS, including prediction and update steps,
can be seen in Algorithm
[3](#alg3 "In 0.F.3 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeNG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").

Input: $`q^{*}_{n-1}(X_{n-1};\lambda_{n-1}^{s}),Y_{n}^{s}`$, maximum
iteration $`I_{max}`$.

1 Output:
$`q^{*}_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})=\prod_{k=1}^{K}q^{*}_{n,k}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$.

2 for *$`k=1,2,...,K`$* do

    3 Prediction step:
$`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n,k})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})`$
using
([10](#S2.E10 "In II-C1 Prediction step in the variational filtering ‣ II-C Variational Filtering for Multi-object Tracking ‣ II Problem Formulation and Modelling ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

4 Initialisation: For $`k=1,2,...,K`$, set
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}(0)=(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1}`$,
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}(0)=-\frac{1}{2}(\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})^{-1}`$.

5 for *$`i=0,1,...,I_{max}`$* do

    6 Exchange variables
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}(i),\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}(i)`$ $`(k=1,2,...,K)`$ with
the current neighbors of sensor $`s`$ in $`\mathcal{N}_{s}(i)`$.

    7 For $`j=1,...,M_{n}`$, compute $`q^{s,*}_{n}(\theta_{n,j})`$ using
([126](#chapterx1.A6.E126 "In 0.F.1 Decentralised gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

    8 Compute the natural gradients
$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}},\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}`$
in
([134](#chapterx1.A6.E134 "In 0.F.3 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeNG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([135](#chapterx1.A6.E135 "In 0.F.3 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeNG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

    9 for *$`k=1,2,...,K`$* do

       10 Update $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}(i+1)`$,
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}(i+1)`$ according to
([132](#chapterx1.A6.E132 "In 0.F.3 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeNG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([133](#chapterx1.A6.E133 "In 0.F.3 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeNG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([42](#S5.E42 "In V-D1 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with diminishing stepsize ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

11 After convergence,
$`q^{*}_{n,k}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n})`$,
where
$`\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n}=-\frac{1}{2}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2})^{-1}\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}`$,
$`\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n}=-\frac{1}{2}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2})^{-1}`$, and
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1},\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}`$ are the final updates of
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}(i),\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}(i)`$.

Algorithm 3 DeNG-VT-DS at time step $`n`$ for each sensor $`s`$

#### 0.F.4 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with gradient tracking (DeNG-VT-GT)

Recall from
([45](#S6.E45 "In VI-A Decentralised Gradient Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
that, for both DeNG-VT-DS and DeNG-VT-GT algorithms, the local estimate
of the optimised variational parameter is defined as
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}=[\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1},\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}]`$
($`k=1,...,K`$), with each $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1},\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}`$
defined in
````
