([53](#S6.E53 "In VI-B2 Derivation of the natural gradient 𝒈_𝑖⁢(ℒ_𝑠) ‣ VI-B Decentralised Natural Gradient Variational Trackers ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
For DeG-VT-GT algorithm, the update of $`\lambda_{n,k}^{s}`$ and
gradient estimates
$`\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s}=[\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,1},\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,2}]`$
for jointly optimising the LM-ELBO $`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})`$ follows
([43](#S5.E43 "In V-D2 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with gradient tracking ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
([44](#S5.E44 "In V-D2 Decentralised (natural) gradient descent variational inference with gradient tracking ‣ V-D Decentralised (Natural) Gradient Descent Variational inference for Maximising LM-ELBO ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).
Specifically, the update equation for each parameter estimate
$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1},\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}`$ and gradient estimates
$`\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,1},\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,2}`$,
$`k=1,...,K`$, at iteration $`i`$ and each sensor $`s`$, are given by

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\lambda_{n,k}^{j,1}(i)+\alpha\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,1}(i)`$ |  | (136) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}(i+1)=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\lambda_{n,k}^{j,2}(i)+\alpha\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,2}(i)`$ |  | (137) |

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,1}(i+1)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{j,1}(i)+\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(i+1))-\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(i)),`$ |  | (138) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,2}(i+1)`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{N_{s}}w_{sj}(i)\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{j,2}(i)+\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(i+1))-\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(i)),`$ |  | (139) |

The full procedure of DeNG-VT-GT, including prediction and update steps,
can be seen in Algorithm
[4](#alg4 "In 0.F.4 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with gradient tracking (DeNG-VT-GT) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks").

Input: $`q^{*}_{n-1}(X_{n-1};\lambda_{n-1}^{s}),Y_{n}^{s}`$, maximum
iteration $`I_{max}`$.

1 Output:
$`q^{*}_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n}^{s})=\prod_{k=1}^{K}q^{*}_{n,k}(X_{n,k};\lambda_{n,k}^{s})`$.

2 for *$`k=1,2,...,K`$* do

$`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n,k})=\mathcal{N}(X_{n,k};\mu^{k*,s}_{n|n-1},\Sigma^{k*,s}_{n|n-1})`$
using

4 Initialisation: Set

$`\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s,2}(0)=\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n,k}^{s}(0))`$.

$`\hat{\bm{\xi}}_{n,k}^{s}(i)`$ $`(k=1,2,...,K)`$ with the current
neighbors of sensor $`s`$ in $`\mathcal{N}_{s}(i)`$.

    7 For $`j=1,...,M_{n}`$, compute $`q^{s,*}_{n}(\theta_{n,j})`$ using

$`\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}},\hat{\nabla}_{\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}}`$
in

([135](#chapterx1.A6.E135 "In 0.F.3 Decentralised natural gradient variational multi-object trackers with with diminishing stepsize (DeNG-VT-DS) ‣ Appendix 0.F Details of proposed trackers and pseudocodes ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

    9 for *$`k=1,2,...,K`$* do

$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}(i+1)`$ according to

$`\lambda_{n,k}^{s,1}(i),\lambda_{n,k}^{s,2}(i)`$.

## Appendix 0.G Handling Non-Convergence: Effective Prior in Decentralised Tracking with Limited Iterations

Here, we will demonstrate that, as discussed in Section
[VI-C](#S6.SS3 "VI-C Robust decentralised tracking: explainable performance in limited iterations ‣ VI Decentralised Gradient-based Variational Multi-object Trackers ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"),
our decentralised (natural) gradient-based variational trackers still
perform sensible inference at time step $`n`$, even when sensors
initially have different predictive priors
$`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})`$ – meaning the inference have not yet
converged at the previous time step $`n-1`$, due to the use of limited
(natural) gradient descent iterations for efficiency. Specifically, we
will show that in this case, the decentralised optimisation at the
current time step $`n`$ still targets the same LM-ELBO in
([37](#S5.E37 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
but with a different prior
$`\hat{p}_{eff}(X_{n})\propto\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})^{1/N_{s}}`$
in place of $`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})`$ in
([37](#S5.E37 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")).

To this end, first recall that our decentralised (natural)
gradient-based variational trackers are designed so that all sensors
collaboratively optimise
$`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})=\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$,
with the LM-ELBO $`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})`$ and local LM-ELBO
$`\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$ defined in
([37](#S5.E37 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
and
([39](#S5.E39 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")),
respectively, as

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})+\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})}\log\frac{p(\theta_{n}|M_{n})}{q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})}+\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})},`$ |  | (140) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{s}(\lambda_{n})=`$ | $`\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})+\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log\frac{p(\theta_{n}^{s}|M_{n}^{s})}{q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}+\frac{1}{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}.`$ |  | (141) |

In cases where sensors have different predictive priors
$`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})`$ ($`s=1,2,...,N_{s}`$) instead of the
identical prior $`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})`$, each sensor essentially computes
the local (natural) gradient with respect to a different local
objective, $`\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$, defined as:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  |  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{s}(\lambda_{n})=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})+\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}\log\frac{p(\theta_{n}^{s}|M_{n}^{s})}{q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n}^{s})}+\frac{1}{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}.`$ |  | (142) |

Consequently, all sensors collaboratively optimise a different objective
$`\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n})`$, which is the sum of local
objectives $`\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$:

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n})`$ | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathcal{L}^{\prime}_{s}(\lambda_{n})`$ |  | (143) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})}\log p(Y_{n}^{s}|\theta_{n}^{s},X_{n})+\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})}\log\frac{p(\theta_{n}|M_{n})}{q_{n}^{*}(\theta_{n})}+\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}`$ |  | (144) |

where the only difference from $`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})`$ in
([37](#S5.E37 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
(or equivalently
([140](#chapterx1.A7.E140 "In Appendix 0.G Handling Non-Convergence: Effective Prior in Decentralised Tracking with Limited Iterations ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks")))
is in the last term, which can be rewritten as follows

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\frac{1}{N_{s}}\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}`$ | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}\sum_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})^{\frac{1}{N_{s}}}}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})^{\frac{1}{N_{s}}}}`$ |  | (145) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}\log\frac{\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})^{\frac{1}{N_{s}}}}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}`$ |  | (146) |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}\log\frac{\hat{p}_{eff}(X_{n})}{q_{n}(X_{n};\lambda_{n})}+C,`$ |  | (147) |

where
$`\hat{p}_{eff}(X_{n})\propto\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})^{1/N_{s}}`$
and
$`C=\log\int\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})^{1/N_{s}}dX_{n}`$
is a constant independent of $`X_{n}`$ or $`\lambda_{n}`$. Since the
constant $`C`$ can be omitted from the objective function
$`\mathcal{L}^{\prime}(\lambda_{n})`$, we conclude that our
decentralised (natural) gradient-based variational trackers still
maximise the same LM-ELBO $`\mathcal{L}(\lambda_{n})`$ in
([37](#S5.E37 "In V-C Decentralisation of LM-ELBO for Multi-sensor Fusion ‣ V Decentralised Gradient-based Variational inference Framework for Sensor Fusion ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))
(or equivalently
([140](#chapterx1.A7.E140 "In Appendix 0.G Handling Non-Convergence: Effective Prior in Decentralised Tracking with Limited Iterations ‣ Decentralised Variational Inference Frameworks for Multi-object Tracking on Sensor Networks"))),
with the only change being the replacement of $`\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n})`$ by
the effective prior
$`\hat{p}_{eff}(X_{n})\propto\prod_{s=1}^{N_{s}}\hat{p}_{n}(X_{n};\eta_{n}^{s})^{1/N_{s}}`$,
which is a reasonable geometric average fusion of individual sensors’
priors. Therefore, the proposed trackers continue to perform sensible
inference at the current time step, even if the sensors’ estimates have
not fully converged at the previous time step $`n-1`$ due to the use of
limited (natural) gradient descent iterations for efficiency.
